什么是邦迪布古病毒病以及为何其致命?

What is Bundibugyo virus disease and why is it lethal?
新闻简报本文讨论了邦迪布古病毒,这是一种罕见的埃博拉病毒病病毒株,曾在刚果民主共和国(DRC)引发了暴发疫情,并传播至肯尼亚。肯尼亚首次确认的病例于2026年10月5日公布,涉及一名曾在DRC居住七年、经乌干达后抵达内罗毕的公民。该患者一个月前在DRC被感染,并接受了治疗治疗,于2026年10月8日在内罗毕医院去世。世界卫生组织(WHO)报告称,邦迪布古病毒是一种严重且常致命的疾病,目前尚无批准的疫苗或治疗治疗方案,且通过与有症状个体的体液直接接触传播。该暴发疫情始于2026年5月,在Ituri省爆发,目前已确认超过8,700例病例,死亡人数超过4,200人。肯尼亚和乌干达的卫生当局正在追踪接触者,包括医疗人员、家庭成员以及Jambojet航班上的乘客,以防止进一步传播。WHO建议采取防护措施,如使用个人防护装备(PPE)、消毒和隔离,以控制该病毒。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该暴发疫情始于2026年5月,肯尼亚首次确认病例是在2026年10月5日。患者于2026年10月8日在内罗毕医院接受治疗治疗时去世。文章发布日期为2026年10月9日。
地点与对象
该暴发疫情起源于DRC的Ituri省,并传播至肯尼亚。患者是一名在DRC居住七年、经乌干达后抵达内罗毕的肯尼亚公民。受影响的人群包括患者本人、医疗工作者、家庭成员以及从坎帕拉飞往内罗毕的Jambojet航班上的23名乘客和4名机组人员。
事件
肯尼亚于2026年10月5日确认了其首例邦迪布古病毒病病例,该病例是在内罗毕医院于2026年10月8日患者去世后公布的。世界卫生组织(WHO)报告称,邦迪布古病毒是一种罕见的埃博拉病毒病毒株,在东非和中非地区引发了暴发疫情。
规模、影响与风险
在DRC的暴发疫情已确认超过8,700例病例,死亡人数超过4,200人。邦迪布古病毒是一种严重且常致命的病毒,目前尚无批准的疫苗或治疗治疗方案。它通过与有症状个体的体液直接接触传播,且不具备空气传播能力。
应对、状态与不确定性
肯尼亚和乌干达的卫生当局正在积极追踪接触者,包括医疗人员、家庭成员以及Jambojet航班上的乘客。WHO强调需要快速隔离、接触追踪和安全埋葬。患者的病例通过实验室检测确认,且正在实施防护措施。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

What is Bundibugyo virus disease and why is it lethal?

News Brief
The article discusses the Bundibugyo virus, a rare strain of Ebola virus disease, which has caused an outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC) and spread to Kenya. The first confirmed case in Kenya was announced on October 5, 2026, involving a citizen who had lived in the DRC for seven years and traveled through Uganda before arriving in Nairobi. The patient, who had been infected a month earlier and received treatment in the DRC, died on October 8, 2026, at Nairobi Hospital. The WHO reports that Bundibugyo is a severe and often fatal disease with no approved vaccine or treatment, and it spreads through direct contact with bodily fluids. The outbreak, which began in May 2026 in Ituri province, has reached over 8,700 confirmed cases and more than 4,200 deaths. Health authorities in Kenya and Uganda are tracing contacts, including medical staff, family members, and passengers on the Jambojet flight, to prevent further spread. The WHO advises protective measures such as PPE, disinfection, and isolation to control the virus.
Original source text (English)
Until very recently, many Kenyans had not heard of Bundibugyo and those who had probably never paid much attention to it until Health Cabinet Secretary Aden Duale announced on October 5 that Kenya had confirmed its first-ever Ebola case. Many know Ebola, as the name has been around for decades, but Bundibugyo has become a trending topic, with online discussions seeking to understand why it is attracting so much attention. According to the World Health Organization (WHO), Bundibugyo is a rare Ebola virus strain with reported outbreaks across East and Central Africa. The outbreak originally began in May 2026 in Ituri province, a northeastern region of the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC). It has since exploded into the largest Bundibugyo Ebola outbreak ever recorded, topping 8,700 confirmed cases and more than 4,200 deaths. Kenya became the fourth country in the region to deal with cases, triggering neighbouring nations such as Uganda, which was declared Ebola-free in August, to put their health systems on high alert. Kenya's lone case was a citizen who had been living in the DRC for seven years and had travelled by land through Uganda before boarding a domestic commercial flight to Nairobi on the night of October 3. Duale said the patient had been infected a month earlier and received treatment in the DRC before travelling, but tragically died days later while receiving treatment at Nairobi Hospital. WHO says Bundibugyo is a severe and often fatal form of Ebola virus disease, with fruit bats suspected to be its natural reservoirs. "Human infection occurs through broken skin or eyes, nose, mouth when handling infected animals, monkeys or bushmeat," WHO said. The disease is not airborne and spreads from person to person through direct contact with the bodily fluids of symptomatic individuals. It takes between two and 21 days for symptoms to appear, and people are not contagious until they actually feel sick. Early signs resemble those of common flu, including fever, fatigue, body aches, headache and sore throat, making the disease difficult to diagnose early. "Later signs worsen to stomach issues, organ failure and sometimes internal and external bleeding," WHO says. The organisation says the disease can only be confirmed through laboratory tests, such as PCR or antibody tests, because its symptoms resemble those of malaria and other tropical diseases. WHO says stopping the spread of the virus requires quick action, including rapid isolation, contact tracing and safe burials. The organisation has advised the public to wash their hands often with soap and water, avoid touching anyone who is sick or their bodily fluids, and seek medical care early at a health facility if unwell. People handling deceased bodies, including healthcare workers without protective gear, are at the greatest risk of infection. "Once identified, protective measures including proper PPE, disinfection and isolation are highly effective," WHO said. The Bundibugyo Ebola virus is particularly concerning because, unlike the more common Zaire strain, there is currently no approved vaccine or virus-specific treatment. Medical teams are relying entirely on intensive supportive care and emergency, early-stage clinical drug trials. Although the current outbreak was reported in the DRC, the virus strain was first discovered in 2007 in the Bundibugyo District of western Uganda. Public anxiety is elevated as health authorities in Kenya and Uganda aggressively trace dozens of high-risk contacts. These include medical staff who treated the deceased patient, family members and the 23 passengers and four crew members who shared the Jambojet flight from Kampala to Nairobi. On Wednesday, Duale said 57 people linked to the deceased patient had been identified and were being managed in accordance with established health protocols
View five news elements
Time
The outbreak began in May 2026 and the first confirmed case in Kenya was reported on October 5, 2026. The patient died on October 8, 2026, while receiving treatment at Nairobi Hospital. The publication date is October 9, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak originated in Ituri province, DRC, and spread to Kenya. The patient was a Kenyan citizen living in the DRC for seven years and traveled through Uganda before arriving in Nairobi. The affected population includes the patient, healthcare workers, family members, and 23 passengers and 4 crew members on the Jambojet flight from Kampala to Nairobi.
Event
Kenya confirmed its first-ever case of Bundibugyo virus disease on October 5, 2026, following the patient's death at Nairobi Hospital on October 8, 2026. The World Health Organization (WHO) reported that Bundibugyo is a rare Ebola virus strain with outbreaks in East and Central Africa.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak in the DRC has reached over 8,700 confirmed cases and more than 4,200 deaths. The Bundibugyo virus is severe and often fatal, with no approved vaccine or treatment. It spreads through direct contact with bodily fluids of symptomatic individuals and is not airborne.
Response, status and uncertainty
Health authorities in Kenya and Uganda are aggressively tracing contacts, including medical staff, family members, and passengers on the Jambojet flight. The WHO emphasizes the need for rapid isolation, contact tracing, and safe burials. The patient's case was confirmed through laboratory tests, and protective measures are being implemented.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

埃博拉疫情更新:刚果民主共和国暴发病例突破8700例,死亡人数达4200例

Ebola Update: DRC Outbreak Passes 8,700 Cases and 4,200 Deaths
新闻简报本文报道了刚果民主共和国(DRC)持续发生的埃博拉暴发疫情,截至2026年10月6日,已确认病例超过8,700例,死亡人数超过4,200例。该暴发疫情由邦迪布古病毒引起,是该国历史上第二大规模的疫情。疫情已在刚果民主共和国的多个省份得到确认,包括班图乌莱、上乌莱、伊图里、北基伏、南基伏、南乌班吉和托肖省。此外,乌干达、肯尼亚和法国也确认有病例。美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)指出,该暴发疫情传播速度比以往更快,并已实施旅行限制和加强筛查措施,以防止病毒进入美国。该机构认为,对美国公众和旅行者的风险较低。CDC积极参与应对工作,有超过500名工作人员在国内外开展行动,以遏制该暴发疫情。由于调查尚未完成且数据尚未标准化,刚果民主共和国的疑似病例暂时不计入总数。
查看新闻五要素
时间
截至2026年10月6日,CDC报告了暴发疫情数据,随后于2026年10月8日发布更新信息,本文于2026年10月10日发表。
地点与对象
该暴发疫情发生在刚果民主共和国(DRC),具体涉及班图乌莱、上乌莱、伊图里、北基伏、南基伏、南乌班吉和托肖省。疫情在DRC之外的乌干达、肯尼亚和法国也得到了确认。
事件
截至2026年10月6日,刚果民主共和国的暴发疫情已确认病例超过8,700例,死亡人数超过4,200例,由邦迪布古病毒引起。CDC指出,该暴发疫情传播速度比以往更快,已成为该国历史上第二大规模的疫情。
规模、影响与风险
刚果民主共和国共有8,728例确认病例和4,205例确认死亡。CDC指出,该暴发疫情是该国第17次疫情,并且在响应启动后40天内病例数就超过了1,000例。该机构还警告称,跨境旅行增加了病毒传播到乌干达、南苏丹、卢旺达和其他地区的风险。美国已限制来自DRC的旅行者入境,并对来自乌干达和南苏丹的旅行者实施了加强筛查。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC积极参与应对工作,有约500名工作人员参与,其中包括超过120名被派往受影响国家的人员。该机构正在努力防止埃博拉病毒传播到美国。由于调查尚未完成且数据尚未标准化,刚果民主共和国的疑似病例暂时不计入总数。CDC认为,对美国公众和旅行者的风险较低。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Ebola Update: DRC Outbreak Passes 8,700 Cases and 4,200 Deaths

News Brief
The article reports on the ongoing Ebola outbreak in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC), which has surpassed 8,700 confirmed cases and 4,200 confirmed deaths as of October 6, 2026. The outbreak is attributed to the Bundibugyo virus, marking it as the second-largest in the DRC's history. Cases have been confirmed in several provinces within the DRC, including Bas-Uele, Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Sud-Kivu, Sud-Ubangi, and Tshopo. Additionally, there are confirmed cases in Uganda, Kenya, and France. The CDC has noted that the outbreak is spreading faster than previous ones and has implemented travel restrictions and enhanced screening measures to prevent the virus from entering the US. The agency considers the risk to the American public and travelers low. The CDC is actively involved in the response, with over 500 staff members working both internationally and domestically to contain the outbreak. Suspected cases in the DRC are temporarily excluded from the count due to pending investigations and data standardization.

Pylori Norovirus Healthcare Associated Infections (HAI) Prevention Antibiotics Immunizations Vaccines Respiratory Infections Community-Acquired Pneumonia Measles Influenza Sexually Transmitted Diseases HIV / AIDS HPV HSV PrEP Skin & Soft Tissue Diseases MRSA Zoonotic & Vector-Borne Diseases Lyme Disease ZIKA Choose Topic Spotlight - Vaccines in America| Antibiotics Deconstructed| Bench to Bedside With SIDP| Antimicrobial Stewardship| Hepatitis Advertisement News|Articles|October 11, 2026 Ebola Update: DRC Outbreak Passes 8,700 Cases and 4,200 Deaths Advertisement The CDC reported 8,750 confirmed cases of Ebola disease and 4,208 confirmed deaths as of October 6, 2026, in an outbreak caused by Bundibugyo virus in the Democratic Republic of the Congo (DRC).1 The agency said the outbreak is spreading substantially faster than previous Ebola outbreaks and is now the second-largest on record. This is the 17th Ebola outbreak in the DRC.1 It surpassed 1,000 confirmed cases within 40 days of response activation, compared with approximately 235 days for the 2018 DRC outbreak to pass the same mark. Case Counts, Geographic Spread, and Cross-Border Cases The DRC accounts for 8,728 confirmed cases and 4,205 confirmed deaths, with no probable cases or deaths included in the official DRC Ministry of Health reporting.1 Cases have been confirmed in Bas-Uele, Haut-Uele, Ituri, Nord-Kivu, Sud-Kivu, Sud-Ubangi, and Tshopo provinces. South Kivu has reported no confirmed cases since May 29. Outside the DRC, Uganda reported 20 confirmed cases, 2 confirmed deaths, 1 probable case, and 1 probable death, with cases reported in the capital of Kampala.1 Kenya reported 1 confirmed case and 1 confirmed death, and France reported 1 confirmed case with no deaths. The last confirmed case in Uganda recovered and was released from care on July 16, 2026, and the last case in France was released on July 4, 2026.1 CDC reports no active cases in either country. Suspected cases and deaths in the DRC have been temporarily excluded from the count pending investigations and data standardization. Learn More Clinical Insights and the Geopolitics Surrounding the Ongoing Ebola Outbreak Response Challenges, Travel Restrictions, and US Risk CDC cited limited health infrastructure, ongoing conflict, violence against health care workers, shortages of personal protective equipment, frequent population movement, and misinformation as factors complicating the response.1 Health care workers are becoming infected while treating patients. The agency warned cross-border travel increases the risk of spread to Uganda, South Sudan, Rwanda, and other areas. To reduce the risk of importation, the US has temporarily restricted entry for travelers who were in the DRC within 21 days of their flight, including US citizens, who will not be allowed to board commercial flights with US destinations.1 Citizens and nationals present in Uganda or South Sudan, and not the DRC, within 21 days must enter through designated airports for enhanced screening. CDC recommends avoiding all travel to Ituri and Nord-Kivu provinces (Level 4) and avoiding nonessential travel to Haut-Uele and Tshopo provinces (Level 3).1 A Level 2 notice advises enhanced precautions in the DRC and Uganda.2 No Ebola cases associated with this outbreak have been reported in the US, and CDC considers the overall risk to the American public and travelers low. About 500 CDC staff are involved in the response, including more than 120 people deployed to affected countries.1 CDC is working internationally and domestically to prevent Ebola from entering the US. Frequently Asked Questions What virus is causing the current Ebola outbreak in the DRC? The outbreak is caused by Bundibugyo virus, a type of Ebola virus. CDC describes it as the 17th Ebola outbreak in the DRC. Is there a risk of Ebola in the United States? No Ebola cases associated with this outbreak have been reported in the US, and CDC considers the likelihood of spread to the US very low. The agency has temporarily restricted entry for travelers recently in the DRC. Which areas should travelers avoid? CDC recommends avoiding all travel to Ituri and Nord-Kivu provinces in the DRC and avoiding nonessential travel to Haut-Uele and Tshopo provinces. Travelers to other parts of the DRC or Uganda should take enhanced precautions and monitor for symptoms for 21 days after leaving. References Ebola Outbreak: Current Situation. CDC. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

NV-387口香糖二期临床试验作为埃博拉病毒治疗进展顺利,NanoViricides公司称

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 Oral Gummies as Treatment of Ebola Progressing Well, Says NanoViricides
新闻简报NanoViricides, Inc. reported on October 13, 2026, that its Phase II clinical trial of NV-387 oral gummies as a treatment for Ebola Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) infection in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) is progressing well. The trial is designed to assess the safety and tolerability of the drug in patients with varying disease severity levels, particularly those in the 'Wet Stage' with severe vomiting and diarrhea. The trial does not include a parallel control group, so efficacy cannot be evaluated in a statistically significant manner. As of October 10, 2026, the DRC outbreak has resulted in 9,000 confirmed cases and 4,339 deaths, with an infection-to-fatality rate (IFR) of 67% to 75%. The US Government is actively preventing the spread of ebolavirus to the general population through travel restrictions and has provided over $1.4 billion in humanitarian assistance. The trial will continue to enroll patients and will determine whether NV-387 is safe enough to proceed to Phase IIB, where its effectiveness will be evaluated in a randomized, controlled setting.
查看新闻五要素
时间
NV-387口香糖作为埃博拉病毒治疗的二期临床试验进展顺利,如NanoViricides公司于2026年10月13日所报告。该试验预计将继续招募不同疾病严重程度的患者,目前重点聚焦于‘干期’和‘湿期’的疫情。
地点与对象
该临床试验正在刚果民主共和国(DRC)进行,特别针对埃博拉邦比布古亚病毒(BDBV)感染的患者。试验将招募处于不同疾病严重程度的个体,预计主要招募处于‘湿期’且有严重呕吐和腹泻症状的患者。
事件
宣布其针对治疗埃博拉邦比布古亚病毒感染的NV-387口香糖二期临床试验进展顺利。该试验旨在评估该药物在不同疾病严重程度患者中的安全性和耐受性。
规模、影响与风险
截至2026年10月10日,DRC当前的BDBV疫情已导致9,000例确诊病例和4,339例死亡。该疫情的感染致死率(IFR)报告为67%至75%。试验预计将继续招募有严重症状的患者,部分患者可能因呕吐而退出。由于缺乏平行对照组,该试验无法以统计学显著的方式评估其疗效。
应对、状态与不确定性
该试验仍在进行中,将继续招募患者。公司将在试验完成后,由主要研究者团队准备报告后发布公告。美国政府正通过旅行限制措施积极防止埃博拉病毒传播至美国一般人群,并已提供超过14亿美元的人道主义援助。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 Oral Gummies as Treatment of Ebola Progressing Well, Says NanoViricides

News Brief
NanoViricides, Inc. reported on October 13, 2026, that its Phase II clinical trial of NV-387 oral gummies as a treatment for Ebola Bundibugyo virus (BDBV) infection in the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC) is progressing well. The trial is designed to assess the safety and tolerability of the drug in patients with varying disease severity levels, particularly those in the 'Wet Stage' with severe vomiting and diarrhea. The trial does not include a parallel control group, so efficacy cannot be evaluated in a statistically significant manner. As of October 10, 2026, the DRC outbreak has resulted in 9,000 confirmed cases and 4,339 deaths, with an infection-to-fatality rate (IFR) of 67% to 75%. The US Government is actively preventing the spread of ebolavirus to the general population through travel restrictions and has provided over $1.4 billion in humanitarian assistance. The trial will continue to enroll patients and will determine whether NV-387 is safe enough to proceed to Phase IIB, where its effectiveness will be evaluated in a randomized, controlled setting.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content This page contains press release content distributed by XPR Media. Members of the editorial and news staff of the USA TODAY Network were not involved in the creation of this content. The placement of this content is part of a paid service. Phase II Clinical Trial of NV-387 Oral Gummies as Treatment of Ebola Progressing Well, Says NanoViricides NanoViricides SHELTON, CT / ACCESS Newswire / October 13, 2026 / NanoViricides, Inc. (NYSE American:NNVC) (the “Company”), a clinical stage leader developing antiviral drugs that viruses cannot escape, said today that its Phase IIA Ebola Treatment Clinical Trial of NV-387 Oral Gummies is progressing well. The Phase IIA part of the clinical trial is designed to evaluate different dosage regimens of the oral gummies for the treatment of different severity levels of the Ebola Bundibugyo virus infection and disease in the current Ebola Outbreak in the Democratic Republic of Congo (“DRC”). The trial is enrolling and is expected to continue enrolling persons exhibiting different disease severity levels, in the “Dry Stage” and “Wet Stage”. The clinical symptoms of the Ebola BDBV disease are complex, and possibly different from prior Ebola virus outbreaks, as explained in the footnote i. Patients are known to come to the Ebola Treatment Centers only when they feel severely ill. Therefore, we are anticipating mostly “Wet Stage” patients, with severe vomiting and diarrhea, to be the primarily enrolled population. Some patients may not be able to take the oral gummies due to vomiting bouts and may be withdrawn; such subjects would not be considered as evaluable if enrolled. The Phase IIA part of the trial is designed to examine the safety and tolerability of NV-387 oral gummies dosing in the Ebola patients, and the number of days that the drug is tolerated within the context of the disease, starting at 6 days of dosing, and extending by 3 day increments to a maximum of 12 days of treatment. The NV-387 treatment will be stopped when the patient’s viral load has become undetectable based on qRT-PCR. Efficacy is not expected to be evaluated in a scientific, statistically significant, manner in such an adaptive treatment scenario. Therefore, and with due ethical consideration, a parallel group of no NV-387 treatment is excluded in this exploratory Phase IIA part. Nevertheless, an efficacy signal can be seen even in this small clinical trial with up to 20 subjects, if NV-387 is highly effective. This can happen if the data of 20 subjects treated with NV-387 is found to be sufficient to statistically discriminate the NV-387 treated group from the general observations of treatment of Ebola patients in the Ebola Treatment Centers in the same area. Ebola Patients in this area have generally experienced an infection-to-fatality rate (IFR) of 67% to 75%. The IFR corrects the data for the fact that the infected person dies on an average about three weeks later, and therefore is a more accurate representation of reality than the crude Case Fatality Rate (CFR) ii. The Company will be able to make an announcement regarding (i) whether NV-387 is found to be sufficiently safe and well tolerated to continue into Phase IIB, and, (ii) if NV-387 group is well discriminated from the general expectation of 67-75% IFR, then whether NV-387 oral gummies treatment demonstrated signs of efficacy against the Ebola Bundibugyo virus; after the Phase IIA clinical trial is completed and a report is prepared by the Principal Investigator’s team for formal communication. After an appropriate NV-387 Oral Gummies dosage regimen is identified, it will be used in an expanded, randomized, controlled clinical trial, Part IIB, to evaluate effectiveness of the Oral treatment drug NV-387 Oral Gummies. NV-387 is designed to attack the virus and reduce the viral load in the body. Reducing viral load correlates with improvement in physiological function. Reduction in viral load to undetected level means complete recovery from the viral disease. However, the bodily functions, may take longer time even after reaching undetectable viral load, as the virus-attacked organs rejuvenate. It is also possible that some permanent damage, e.g. kidney damage, may persist even after virological recovery. The current BDBV outbreak has continued to expand, with 9,000 confirmed cases and 4,339 deaths as of October 10, 2026, according to the WHO iii, with all but a few cases in DRC. An Ebola patient was able to travel from DRC via Uganda to Kenya, and died at home there recently, despite travel restrictions. Fortunately after a week, no secondary infections from this patient have been detected (yet). This emphasizes the potential risk to the world, although the possibility of an Ebola outbreak in the USA or globally remains negligible, because of various global travel restrictions. Within DRC, while the initial outbreak sites including Ituri have possibly stabilized, the outbreak has started gaining strength in Nord Kivu province with 40% of national confirmed cases now coming from Nord Kivu, which does not have sufficient ebola treatment centers capacity, with only 29 beds in treatment center, and only about 20 “transit facility” beds for holding suspected cases until diagnosis results come in, according to Doctors Without Borders iv. Recently, thousands of refugees fled from a refugee camp where more than 30 people had died from Ebola BDBV, to escape from security forces that were investigating weapons stored in the camp. Their fleeing has increased the risk of spread v. Additionally, 6 new Ebola cases were recorded in an Eastern part of DRC controlled by rebel groups, further complicating efforts to control the outbreak vi. The USA has been, laudably, and proudly, the largest provider of humanitarian and relief assistance in this outbreak, with almost $1.4 Billion in contributions. The USA has already provided $887 Million, and has pledged another $500 Million, of which $267 million has been released, according to the US Department of State vii. There is no current threat of Ebola in the USA. The US Government is active in ensuring that suspected or confirmed ebolavirus cases do not enter the general population in the USA. To this end, travel from DRC has been restricted, with pre-travel quarantine requirements imposed, and suspect travelers are directed to screening at specific airports and may be further quarantined. Travelers going to and from Central Africa need to constantly check travel restrictions as well as travel limitations in light of these changing outbreak conditions. It is apparent that travel restrictions have been able to control the outbreak periphery to DRC alone (with few early cases in Uganda) minimizing risk to the rest of the world. There is no approved Treatment or Vaccine for the new variant of the Bundibugyo Ebolavirus (BDBV) that is causing the current rapidly expanding outbreak of the Ebolavirus Disease (EVD) in DRC. The rare Bundibugyo strain of Ebola virus causing the current outbreak appears to be its new variant, likely freshly introduced from some animal source viii, such as fruit bats. NV-387 is a broad-spectrum antiviral that mimics the host-side features that the virus requires, and is likely to be effective against Ebola viruses because they use the same host-side feature mimicked by NV-387. NV-387 Oral Gummies is a drug product readily delivered orally. It does not even require swallowing effort or water, because it dissolves in the mouth by itself, simplifying delivery for even sick individuals with swallowing difficulties. This oral delivery is an important feature that puts NV-387, a broad-spectrum antiviral, as being superior to the other approaches. “Only safe and effective broad-spectrum antiviral drugs like NV-387 that can effectively tackle most viral infections will enable the world to combat viruses and defend the global population in the war against known and unknown nanoscopic enemies that are viruses,” commented Dr. Diwan, adding, “Today, NV-387 is the only drug in clinical development with such broad-spectrum potential that promises to combat diverse epidemics like Mpox and Ebola, and even Measles, to the best of our knowledge.” The outbreak which was declared a Public Health Emergency of International Concern (“PHEIC”) by the WHO on May 17, 2026, continues to rapidly expand, outpacing containment efforts. The outbreak arose in a high traffic region bordering the Democratic Republic of Congo (DRC), with travel contacts to Uganda, and South Sudan and with 11 more nations in Africa at risk ix. NV-387 is a broad-spectrum antiviral that mimics the host-side feature called heparan sulfate proteoglycan (HSPG) that over 90-95% of human pathogenic viruses require for infecting cells. No matter how much the virus changes in the field, it continues to use HSPG, and therefore it cannot escape the drug NV-387. In contrast, Remdesivir is a small molecule inhibitor of the viral RDRP enzyme needed for making copies of the viral genome, and the virus can possibly escape by small number of mutations. All Ebola viruses utilize HSPG as the attachment receptor prior to gaining entry into the cell. Thereafter, followed by entry into the cell inside endosomes, the ebolavirus surface glycoprotein is substantially degraded, opening up its site for binding to its cognate receptor called NPC1, thereby entering into the cytoplasm where the next steps in its replication begin. Thus there is a strong rationale that NV-387 could be highly effective against Ebola virus infections, not just Bundibugyo, but also the Sudan and other viruses for which there are no treatments. All previous anti-Ebola efforts have been focused on vaccines and antibodies x. This has led to approval of therapies that are specific to the Ebolavirus Zaire strain only, albeit with limited effectiveness. This leaves out all other filoviruses of consequence: Sudan, Marburg, and the more rare Bundibugyo with no treatment or vaccine. In contrast, if NV-387, as a broad-spectrum antiviral, is found to be effective against the Bundibugyo virus, it will likely be effective against all ebolaviruses and possibly all filoviruses; that would be a game changer for pandemic preparedness. At present, BDBV has been consistently demonstrating high crude CFR of 48% and IFR of 67% in DRC. Therefore, BDBV is of great concern as a potential pandemic disease. However, it is believed that ebolaviruses do not transmit via respiratory droplets or aerosols and rather require extensive contact with bodily fluids of an infected person. In addition, within DRC and internationally, certain protective quarantine measures for travel from the outbreak areas have been implemented. Therefore, currently there is no apparent threat of a global pandemic. With ever-increasing global travel, local outbreaks such as ebola can quickly travel far and wide potentially causing global pandemics, as was the case with COVID-19, if not caught in time. It is not feasible to produce a new vaccine and a new set of antibody drugs to combat every possible virus. Even if vaccines and antibodies are produced, the virus would escape by generating variants, as the world has witnessed during the COVID-19 pandemic. ABOUT NANOVIRICIDES NanoViricides, Inc. (the “Company”) (www.nanoviricides.com) is a clinical stage company that is creating special purpose nanomaterials for antiviral therapy. The Company’s novel nanoviricideTM class of drug candidates and the nanoviricideTM technology are based on intellectual property, technology and proprietary know-how of TheraCour Pharma, Inc. The Company has a Memorandum of Understanding with TheraCour for the development of drugs based on these technologies for all antiviral infections. The MoU does not include cancer and similar diseases that may have viral origin but require different kinds of treatments. The Company has obtained broad, exclusive, sub-licensable, field licenses to drugs developed in several licensed fields from TheraCour Pharma, Inc. The Company’s business model is based on licensing technology from TheraCour Pharma Inc. for specific application verticals of specific viruses, as established at its foundation in 2005. Our lead drug candidate is NV-387, a broad-spectrum antiviral drug that we plan to develop as a treatment of RSV, COVID, Long COVID, Influenza, and other respiratory viral infections, as well as MPOX/Smallpox infections. Our other advanced drug candidate is NV-HHV-1 for the treatment of Shingles. The Company cannot project an exact date for filing an IND for any of its drugs because of dependence on a number of external collaborators and consultants. The Company is currently focused on advancing NV-387 into Phase II human clinical trials. NV-CoV-2 (API NV-387) is our nanoviricide drug candidate for COVID-19 that does not encapsulate remdesivir. NV-CoV-2-R is our other drug candidate for COVID-19 that is made up of NV-387 with remdesivir encapsulated within its polymeric micelles. The Company believes that since remdesivir is already US FDA approved, our drug candidate encapsulating remdesivir is likely to be an approvable drug, if safety is comparable. Remdesivir is developed by Gilead. The Company has developed both of its own drug candidates NV-CoV-2 and NV-CoV-2-R independently. The Company is also developing drugs against a number of viral diseases including oral and genital Herpes, viral diseases of the eye including EKC and herpes keratitis, H1N1 swine flu, H5N1 bird flu, seasonal Influenza, HIV, Hepatitis C, Rabies, Dengue fever, and Ebola virus, among others. NanoViricides’ platform technology and programs are based on the TheraCour® nanomedicine technology of TheraCour, which TheraCour licenses from AllExcel. NanoViricides holds a worldwide exclusive perpetual license to this technology for several drugs with specific targeting mechanisms in perpetuity for the treatment of the following human viral diseases: Human Immunodeficiency Virus (HIV/AIDS), Hepatitis B Virus (HBV), Hepatitis C Virus (HCV), Rabies, Herpes Simplex Virus (HSV-1 and HSV-2), Varicella-Zoster Virus (VZV), Influenza and Asian Bird Flu Virus, Dengue viruses, Japanese Encephalitis virus, West Nile Virus, Ebola/Marburg viruses, and certain Coronaviruses. The Company intends to obtain a license for RSV, Poxviruses, and/or Enteroviruses if the initial research is successful. As is customary, the Company must state the risk factor that the path to typical drug development of any pharmaceutical product is extremely lengthy and requires substantial capital. As with any drug development efforts by any company, there can be no assurance at this time that any of the Company’s pharmaceutical candidates would show sufficient effectiveness and safety for human clinical development. Further, there can be no assurance at this time that successful results against coronavirus in our lab will lead to successful clinical trials or a successful pharmaceutical product. The trial is expected to continue enrolling patients exhibiting different disease severity levels, with the current focus on the 'Dry Stage' and 'Wet Stage' of the outbreak.

The trial is enrolling individuals who have reached different severity levels of the disease, with an expectation of mostly 'Wet Stage' patients who have severe vomiting and diarrhea.
